=== PROMPT ===
You will be given a competitive programming problem.
A positive integer not less than 10 whose top digit (the most significant digit) in decimal representation is strictly larger than every other digit in that number is called a Snake number.
For example, 31 and 201 are Snake numbers, but 35 and 202 are not.
Find how many Snake numbers exist between L and R, inclusive.

Input

The input is given from Standard Input in the following format:
L R

Output

Print the answer.

Constraints


- 10 \leq L \leq R \leq 10^{18}
- All input values are integers.

Sample Input 1

97 210

Sample Output 1

6

The Snake numbers between 97 and 210, inclusive, are 97, 98, 100, 200, 201, and 210: there are six.

Sample Input 2

1000 9999

Sample Output 2

2025

Sample Input 3

252509054433933519 760713016476190692

Sample Output 3

221852052834757

Use the following starter code to write the final solution.
```python

```

Reason in a commit-implement-recheck way.

A productive rhythm for this task usually looks like this:
- Isolate the decisive constraints, the target complexity, and at most one or two approaches that are still truly plausible.
- If two candidates remain, compare them only on the decisive constraint and commit quickly to the stronger one instead of keeping a wide search open.
- Before coding, keep only a tiny implementation ledger for the chosen plan: main invariant, key state or transition, and the one or two edge cases most likely to fail.
- Move into code as soon as that ledger is clear. After coding starts, keep reasoning attached to the current draft rather than reopening the high-level search.
- After the first complete draft, do one short implementation-check-adjust-check loop on the same draft: test the key edge case, patch locally if needed, and recheck.
- Reopen the main design only if a concrete counterexample breaks the core invariant. Otherwise prefer the local patch and keep closing.
- Once the current draft, invariant, and short recheck still agree, finalize the solution.

Try to avoid patterns like these:
- spending too long comparing nearby plans before committing to one workable approach;
- returning from code to broad algorithm discussion without a concrete contradiction;
- turning one local doubt into a full restart when the current draft only needs a small patch.

Output only the final Python code enclosed in a code block.

import sys

def count_snake_upto(n):
    if n < 10:
        return 0
    s = str(n)
    length = len(s)
    first = ord(s[0])-48
    total = 0
    # all shorter lengths
    # precompute powers? Could compute exact counts:
    for k in range(2, length):  # k digits
        cnt = 0
        for a in range(1,10):
            cnt += a ** (k-1)
        total += cnt
    # first digit less than first
    for a in range(1, first):
        total += a ** (length-1)
    # first digit equal first, count suffixes
    # suffix s[1:]
    # allowed digits 0..first-1
    F = first
    m = length - 1
    # precompute powers F^remaining maybe
    # We can scan suffix left to right; need F^(remaining) for remaining positions after current.
    # Since m <=18, compute pow(F, remaining) each time or precompute list.
    powF = [1]*(m+1)
    for i in range(1,m+1):
        powF[i] = powF[i-1]*F
    # powF[r] = F^r
    for i, ch in enumerate(s[1:]):
        d = ord(ch)-48
        rem = m - i - 1
        if d >= F:
            # all F allowed digits are less than d
            total += F * powF[rem]
            # no need continue; all valid suffixes are already counted
            return total
        else:
            # digits 0..d-1 are allowed and less than d
            total += d * powF[rem]
            # if choose digit equal d, continue
            # if d == 0, no less choices, continue
    # all suffix digits equal to N's suffix and <F
    total += 1
    return total

Let's design final code:

import sys

def count_upto(n):
    if n < 10:
        return 0
    s = str(n)
    length = len(s)
    first = int(s[0])
    ans = 0

    # Count all Snake numbers with fewer digits
    for k in range(2, length):
        for a in range(1, 10):
            ans += a ** (k - 1)

    # Count length == length, first digit smaller than first
    for a in range(1, first):
        ans += a ** (length - 1)

    # Count length == length, first digit == first
    m = length - 1
    # powers of first for suffix positions
    pow_first = [1] * (m + 1)
    for i in range(1, m + 1):
        pow_first[i] = pow_first[i - 1] * first

    suffix = s[1:]
    for i, ch in enumerate(suffix):
        d = ord(ch) - 48
        rem = m - i - 1
        if d >= first:
            ans += first * pow_first[rem]
            return ans
        else:
            ans += d * pow_first[rem]
    ans += 1
    return ans

def main():
    L,R = map(int, sys.stdin.readline().split())
    print(count_upto(R)-count_upto(L-1))

if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()

Let's think if code with exact list:
EXACT = [0] * 20
for length in range(2, 20):
    EXACT[length] = sum(a ** (length - 1) for a in range(1, 10))

def count_upto(n):
    if n < 10:
        return 0
    s = str(n)
    length = len(s)
    first = ord(s[0]) - 48
    ans = sum(EXACT[2:length])  # lengths 2..length-1
    for a in range(1, first):
        ans += a ** (length - 1)
    m = length - 1
    pow_first = [1] * (m + 1)
    for i in range(1, m + 1):
        pow_first[i] = pow_first[i - 1] * first
    for i, ch in enumerate(s[1:]):
        d = ord(ch) - 48
        rem = m - i - 1
        if d >= first:
            ans += first * pow_first[rem]
            return ans
        ans += d * pow_first[rem]
    ans += 1
    return ans

Let's perhaps write final code:

```python
import sys

MAX_LEN = 20
EXACT = [0] * MAX_LEN
for length in range(2, MAX_LEN):
    EXACT[length] = sum(a ** (length - 1) for a in range(1, 10))

def count_upto(n):
    if n < 10:
        return 0
    s = str(n)
    length = len(s)
    first = ord(s[0]) - ord('0')

    ans = sum(EXACT[2:length])

    for a in range(1, first):
        ans += a ** (length - 1)

    m = length - 1
    pow_first = [1] * (m + 1)
    for i in range(1, m + 1):
        pow_first[i] = pow_first[i - 1] * first

    for i, ch in enumerate(s[1:]):
        d = ord(ch) - ord('0')
        rem = m - i - 1
        if d >= first:
            ans += first * pow_first[rem]
            return ans
        ans += d * pow_first[rem]

    ans += 1
    return ans

def main():
    data = sys.stdin.read().split()
    if not data:
        return
    L = int(data[0])
    R = int(data[1])
    print(count_upto(R) - count_upto(L - 1))

if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()
```

Now, let's maybe think if there is any issue with "count_upto" if n=10^18 and R=10^18, L=999999999999998083, answer1. Good.

Now, let's maybe think if there is any issue with "count_upto" if n=10^18 and R=10^
</think>

=== ANSWER ===
```python
import sys

MAX_LEN = 20
EXACT = [0] * MAX_LEN
for length in range(2, MAX_LEN):
    EXACT[length] = sum(a ** (length - 1) for a in range(1, 10))


def count_upto(n):
    if n < 10:
        return 0

    s = str(n)
    length = len(s)
    first = ord(s[0]) - 48

    ans = sum(EXACT[2:length])

    for a in range(1, first):
        ans += a ** (length - 1)

    m = length - 1
    pow_first = [1] * (m + 1)
    for i in range(1, m + 1):
        pow_first[i] = pow_first[i - 1] * first

    for i, ch in enumerate(s[1:]):
        d = ord(ch) - 48
        rem = m - i - 1

        if d >= first:
            ans += first * pow_first[rem]
            return ans

        ans += d * pow_first[rem]

    ans += 1
    return ans


def main():
    data = sys.stdin.read().split()
    L = int(data[0])
    R = int(data[1])
    print(count_upto(R) - count_upto(L - 1))


if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()
```
